### CARD-0151 · [idea] [core] Remote creation of kanban cards from phone
**Status:** Done

**Raised 2026-08-12 05:48 MST**, from Joseph wanting to open a new kanban card while away from this machine (from his phone), rather than needing to be sitting at a Claude Code session.

**First approach tried: Claude Code cloud routines (interview-driven, matching the original ask).** Created a routine via the `RemoteTrigger` API — a saved, on-demand cloud Claude Code session with a self-contained prompt (read `CLAUDE.md`/`kanban-board.md` for conventions, interview Joseph including fetching any URL he shares via `WebFetch`, write the card, open a PR via `gh`). Hit and resolved a real blocker along the way: creation failed with "Connect your GitHub account" until Joseph installed the Claude GitHub App (`claude.ai/code/onboarding?magic=github-app-setup`) — the account-level "connect" he'd done first wasn't the same as actually installing the App against the repo.

**Abandoned after real use — too clunky for the actual use case.** Once working, reaching the routine from a phone required: open browser → sign in to claude.ai → navigate to Claude Code → Routines → find the routine → tap Run. A bookmarked direct link was offered to cut most of those steps, but Joseph's real ask, once he'd felt the friction, turned out to be different from what was interviewed originally: **most of the time he just wants to drop a placeholder — often triggered by reading an article he wants attached as reference — and do the real interview later, here, not conduct a full remote interview on his phone at all.**

**Redesigned around existing infrastructure instead of building new.** CARD-0128's `open_finding_pr()` (`core/maintenance/open_kanban_pr.py`) already does exactly the "placeholder stub + PR" half — used today by the Pi/M8 update-check scripts, pure GitHub REST API, no `gh` CLI needed, reuses the already-provisioned GitHub PAT (`/etc/jctsh/github.env`). Only needed a new trigger: an email instead of a maintenance-check finding. Interviewed: Gmail plus-addressing (`joscthomas+kbc@gmail.com`) as the recognition mechanism, subject as the card's title/one-liner and body as additional detail (both just flow into `open_finding_pr()`'s single `message` argument), polled from the Pi every 30 minutes.

**Credential detour, real and not small.** Gmail App Passwords turned out to be unavailable on this account even with 2FA on (Google's been phasing them out) — ruled out plain IMAP. Pivoted to Gmail API OAuth2: Joseph created a Google Cloud project + OAuth client (Desktop app type); a one-time local authorization bootstrap script was written to capture the redirect and exchange it for a refresh token. Hit a real snag mid-flow — the redirect landed on `localhost:8765`, but that port was already occupied by an unrelated local dev server on Joseph's machine, so the listener never caught it; recovered by manually extracting the `code` from the page Joseph saw and exchanging it directly via a one-off script instead. Then a second real finding: the first refresh token minted (while the OAuth app was still in "Testing" publish status) carried a `refresh_token_expires_in` of ~7 days — a known Google restriction for sensitive scopes on unverified apps, confirmed by comparing it against an identical token minted immediately after Joseph published the app, which had no expiry field at all. Publishing was the right call, at the cost of Google's standard "unverified app" warning on the one-time consent screen (bypassed via the developer-only "Advanced → Go to... (unsafe)" link, expected/normal for a personal app that hasn't gone through formal verification).

**Built and deployed 2026-08-12:** `core/maintenance/email-idea-check.py` (+ matching `.service`/`.timer`, mirroring `pi-maintenance-check`'s existing systemd pattern exactly) — pure `urllib`, no new dependencies, mints a fresh Gmail API access token from the stored refresh token each run, searches `to:kbc is:unread`, and for each match calls `open_finding_pr()` with a fresh empty state dict per email (dedup is the Gmail `UNREAD` label itself, removed only after a successful PR open, not `open_finding_pr()`'s own single-fingerprint memory — that mechanism is built for "same finding repeated across polls," not "many distinct one-off emails"). Credentials in `/etc/jctsh/email-idea-check.env`, root-owned/600, matching `github.env`'s existing pattern. Timer enabled (every 30 min).

**Verified against real behavior, not just "deployed with no errors":** first run (before any test email existed) correctly found nothing and exited clean, confirming the OAuth/API path itself worked. Joseph then forwarded a real article (a Hackster.io lithium-battery-charger circuit page) to the plus-address — triggered manually rather than waiting for the timer, opened a real PR: **[#9](https://github.com/joscthomas/jctsh/pull/9)**, confirmed via the GitHub API to be open, based on `main`, containing the forwarded email's subject and full body in the stub.

**Real bug found during that same verification, fixed at the source.** PR #9's title came out garbled: `"CARD-XXX: Fwd: ...Hackster.io\n\n-"` — `open_finding_pr()`'s title construction blindly sliced `message[:72]`/`message[:80]` with no awareness of line breaks, so it cut straight through the `subject\n\nbody` boundary into the start of the forwarded email's own header block. Fixed in the shared module (`core/maintenance/open_kanban_pr.py`) with a `_title_line()` helper that derives the title from the message's first line only, then truncates — a no-op for the three existing callers (all already pass single-line messages), only changing behavior for multi-line messages like this new caller's. Deployed to the Pi and confirmed syntax-valid there. **Not yet deployed to the M8** (`photo-server`) — this session has no SSH access to that host (only the Pi has passwordless key auth set up), so `immich-update-check.py`/`container-update-check.py`/`maintenance-check.py` there are still running the old blind-truncation version. Low urgency (cosmetic, only bites when a finding message happens to be long and multi-line, which the M8 scripts' own findings haven't been so far) but worth fixing next time that host is reachable.

**Loose end from the first approach:** the abandoned Claude Code routine did fire once for real (`last_fired_at` shows activity) — almost certainly Joseph tapping "Run" while exploring the clunky web UI before the pivot. Routine is now disabled (routines can't be deleted via the API) so it won't fire again; if that one run started an interview session somewhere, it was never followed up on and is assumed harmless/abandoned.

**Still needed:** add the new 30-minute email-check timer to `jctsh-network.md`'s Scheduled Maintenance Windows table for visibility, matching how every other recurring job on the Pi/M8 is tracked there.

**Done when:** revised from the original (interview-driven remote session) to match what Joseph actually wanted once he'd tried the alternative — from his phone, emailing an idea (subject + optional body, optionally forwarding an article as reference) reliably produces a real, correctly-formatted placeholder-stub PR against `kanban-board.md` within the poll interval, with the real interview/scoping pass happening later in a normal session. **Met**, verified against a real forwarded email producing real PR #9.

**Related:** CARD-0128 (`open_finding_pr()`/`resolve_and_merge()`, the infrastructure this reuses), `core/maintenance/open_kanban_pr.py`, `core/maintenance/email-idea-check.py`, `jctsh-network.md` (pending maintenance-window entry).

### CARD-0150 · [bug] [homeassistant] Samsung TV was on when we got home — investigate and fix
**Status:** Done

**Raised 2026-08-12 05:48 MST**, from Joseph finding the Samsung TV on when he and family returned from the airport last night (~2026-08-11 22:30 MST) — no known reason for it to have turned on. Not yet confirmed whether the TV is even integrated into HA/SmartThings as a controllable/queryable entity; that's the first thing to check.

**Investigation starting point:** Joseph's own suspicion, unconfirmed — the HA light automation routine (whichever one runs around arrival/dusk) is "the only thing I can think of that was different" around that time. Worth checking `core/homeassistant/automations.yaml` for anything that could plausibly cross-trigger the TV (e.g. a scene, a SmartThings routine bundling lights+TV, an HDMI-CEC signal from another device the automation does control) — not assumed to be the actual cause, just the lead to start from. TV is also referred to as "Groom TV."

**Scope:**
1. Determine whether the TV is an HA/SmartThings entity at all; if so pull its on/off history around 2026-08-11 22:30 MST from HA logbook/history.
2. Identify what actually turned it on (an HA automation, a SmartThings routine, HDMI-CEC from another device, a TV-native schedule/feature, etc.).
3. Once root cause is confirmed, implement a fix so it doesn't happen again (exact fix depends on what's found — could be an automation condition, a SmartThings routine edit, disabling a TV-side feature, etc.).

**Investigated 2026-08-12 06:10 MST, real HA recorder DB + JCTsh component logs on the Pi, not guessed:**
- Entity is `media_player.groom_tv` — exposed via HA's **Google Cast integration**, not SmartThings. (`binary_sensor.samsung_tv_is_present`/`device_tracker.samsung_tv_is_home` are separate NetAlertX network-presence entities per CARD-0078, unrelated to control.)
- Arrival time independently confirmed via garage-radar presence events: 22:27:49–22:33:53 MST — matches Joseph's recollection.
- **HA automation is ruled out as the cause.** Queried every `call_service` event HA logged that entire day (midnight–6am next day): only Traveling Lights (`light.pendants`/`light.nook`, on 19:35 off 22:08 — Joseph's suspected lead), garage-presence switch, and Front Porch notifications ever ran. Zero service calls touched `media_player`, `cast`, `smartthings`, or `scene` domains all day, and `automations.yaml` has no TV reference anywhere. Traveling Lights only ever targets the two lights.
- No local power blip either — checked JCTsh component logs (`/mnt/jctsh-logs/jctsh.log`) across the same window for reconnect/reboot events on any device; none found.
- One red herring investigated and dismissed: the TV's network-presence sensor was continuously "home" from 03:36 MST Aug 11 through 22:35:56 (disconnecting ~5 min after arrival, consistent with Joseph powering it off) — but the same always-connected pattern with a ~30min blip near 3am repeats on other nights too, so it's normal WiFi-standby behavior, not a signal of when the display itself turned on.
- Confirmed with Joseph: no TV-native on-timer or SmartThings-app routine configured, and no one else had access to the house/TV during the trip (rules out a person).

**Trip window corrected 2026-08-12 06:25 MST — travel was July 25 to Aug 11, 17 days, not just one evening.** Re-scoped the investigation to the full trip rather than just the arrival hour:
- There **is** a physical Chromecast plugged into the TV via HDMI (Joseph confirmed) — `media_player.groom_tv` is that Chromecast's HA entity, not a TV-native integration. A Chromecast can wake a TV via HDMI-CEC and switch input the moment anything casts to it, independent of any Google Home smart-home linking or Routine.
- Checked directly in the Google Home app (Joseph): the Chromecast's own Activity/cast history shows **nothing** around the arrival window. Also confirmed no Google Home automations reference the TV at all.
- **No power outage during the full July 25–Aug 11 window.** Checked every ESP32 component's heartbeat uptime counter (which resets to a low value on any reboot) across the entire trip in `/mnt/jctsh-logs/jctsh.log`/`.log.1` — none show a reset; `garage-radar`, `salt-sensor`, `front-porch-temp-sensor` all have continuous uptime spanning well before July 25 through today. (The Pi itself did reboot once, 2026-08-10 03:00 MST via `journalctl --list-boots`, but since no ESP32 device rebooted alongside it, that reads as a routine/scheduled Pi reboot, not a house-wide power event — rules out the "power restored, TV defaults on" theory.)
- Confirmed with Joseph: TV was off when they left on July 25 (not a case of it simply never being turned off).

**Correction 2026-08-12 06:40 MST — the "no Cast activity all day" read above was wrong; it was a data gap, not evidence of nothing happening.** Pulled `media_player.groom_tv`'s *full* history (not just the incident window): it's normally very active — 800+ state records since Aug 3, cycling `playing`/`idle`/`paused`/`off` constantly on a typical day (confirmed real activity happening again as of this morning, Aug 12 08:57 onward). But for the incident window specifically, the last recorded row before the trip's end is `off` at **Aug 11 03:06 MST**, then **literally nothing** — no rows at all — until `unavailable`/`off` at **Aug 12 03:23 MST**, over 24 hours later. That's not "recorded off the whole time," it's a genuine recording blackout spanning the entire arrival window. HA's own history for this entity is therefore **inconclusive** for this incident, not exculpatory — it can neither confirm nor rule out when the TV actually turned on.

**Investigation is now inconclusive from every source checkable remotely** — HA automations (real event-log check, still solid), JCTsh/ESP32 logs, Google Home cast activity, TV-native timers, SmartThings routines, and other-person access have all been checked and ruled out, with no trigger event found in the 17-day window. HA's own Cast history for the TV, the one place that might have shown something, has an unexplained 24-hour gap covering exactly the relevant time. This points toward a TV-side or Chromecast-side spontaneous wake (both Samsung Anynet+/CEC glitches and Chromecast standby/update-check CEC signals are known, documented causes of unprompted TV power-on that leave no trace in Google Home's activity log or HA) — plausible but not provable with the telemetry available.

**Joseph's call 2026-08-12 06:50 MST: pivot to detection/alerting rather than keep chasing an unfindable root cause.** Re-scoped `Done when` accordingly (see below).

**Design, interviewed:** a new HA automation gated on the existing `automation.traveling_lights_evening_on` (the CARD-0098 Traveling Lights toggle Joseph already flips on/off around trips) so it only fires while nobody's expected home. Keys off `media_player.groom_tv`'s Cast-integration state crossing between off/unavailable/unknown and an in-use state (playing/idle/paused/buffering). Two directions, per Joseph's explicit follow-up ask: notify when the TV turns **on** unexpectedly, and notify when it turns **off** again (the expected response once Joseph turns it off remotely via Google Home after seeing the "on" alert) — so he gets confirmation the remote power-off actually took, not just the initial warning. Also, per Joseph's ask, the existing Traveling Lights on/off notifications (the routine ones already sent when the presence-simulation lights toggle) now append the TV's current state to their message text, as a passive check-in independent of the dedicated alert.

**Built and deployed 2026-08-12 07:05 MST:**
- New automation `Traveling Mode - Unexpected TV Activity` (`automation.traveling_mode_unexpected_tv_activity`) in `core/homeassistant/automations.yaml` — two triggers tagged `on`/`off` (mirroring Traveling Lights' own trigger-id/choose-block pattern), condition on the Traveling Lights toggle, push notifications to both phones (`mobile_app_pixel_10_pro_xl`, `mobile_app_pixel_7_pro`) for each direction.
- Existing `Traveling Lights` automation's four notify actions (on x2 phones, off x2 phones) extended to append `TV: {{ states('media_player.groom_tv') }}.` to the message text.
- YAML syntax-checked locally (`python -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(...)"`) before deploying, `scp`'d to `/home/pi/homeassistant/automations.yaml`, HA container restarted (no long-lived API token available in this session to use the lighter-weight `automation.reload` service, so a full `docker restart homeassistant` was used instead — same net effect for picking up config changes). Confirmed clean: container reports `healthy`, no config-load errors in `docker logs` (excluding known pre-existing unrelated noise — SmartThings light KeyError, Bluetooth scanner errors, a Chromecast connect failure for an unrelated speaker — none from this change), and the new automation shows up in the entity registry (`automation.traveling_mode_unexpected_tv_activity`, not disabled).

**Joseph's live test, 2026-08-12 07:15 MST — both directions confirmed working, and it immediately surfaced a real bug.** Turned the TV off: got "Groom TV turned off (off)." Turned it back on: got "Groom TV turned on (idle) while traveling." — mechanism validated end-to-end, the gap noted above (no API token for a synthetic test) is moot now that a real test happened. But a 3rd, unprompted notification arrived — "Groom TV turned off (unavailable)" — **while the TV was still on.** Pulled the exact state timeline from the recorder DB to confirm: `media_player.groom_tv` dropped from `playing` straight to `unavailable` at 09:47:46, then recovered to `idle`/`playing` by 09:48:54 — a genuine ~67s Cast-integration connectivity blip during confirmed active use, not a real power change. That recovery would also have matched the 'on' trigger's criteria, so a false follow-up "turned on" alert was likely imminent too — the entity is noisier than the design accounted for.

**Fix, interviewed and built 2026-08-12 07:20 MST:** two changes, both per Joseph's direction —
1. Both notification messages now label the parenthetical explicitly as `(Chromecast status: ...)` rather than a bare state value, so it's clear this is the Chromecast's own reported status, not a literal TV-power reading.
2. **Debounce redesigned, not just added.** First instinct was a trigger-level `for: 00:02:00`, but that was caught and rejected before deploying: HA's `for` requires the *exact same state value* to hold the whole time, and this entity legitimately churns between playing/idle/paused/buffering several times a second during real use — a same-value `for` would rarely if ever be satisfied during genuine use, silently breaking the 'on' alert. Replaced with a delay-then-recheck-category pattern instead: each branch fires immediately on the category transition, waits 2 minutes, then re-checks the entity is *still* in the new category (off-ish or on-ish, any value within it) before actually notifying. This tolerates the normal churn while still filtering the observed blip (the 09:47:46 `unavailable` had recovered to `idle` well within the 2-minute window, so a repeat of that exact scenario would now correctly send nothing).

**Deployed and verified clean 2026-08-12 07:25 MST:** YAML syntax-checked locally, `scp`'d, HA container restarted (`docker restart homeassistant` — no long-lived API token available in this session for the lighter `automation.reload`), confirmed `healthy` with no config-load errors and the automation still registered/enabled (`automation.traveling_mode_unexpected_tv_activity`).

**Joseph's second live test, 2026-08-12 07:35 MST — off then back on, "just like last time" — produced zero notifications, exposing a second real bug.** Pulled the exact timeline again: TV went `playing` → `off` at 10:01:46 (starting the 'off' branch's 2-minute delay), then bounced back to `idle` just 14 seconds later at 10:02:00. Two compounding failures under `mode: single`: (1) the 'on' transition at 10:02:00 fired while the 'off' branch's delay was still in progress — `single` mode silently *drops* a new trigger event while already running, it does not queue it, so the 'on' notification never had a chance; (2) the 'off' branch's own delayed recheck (due ~10:03:46) found the TV back on by then, so its condition failed too — no 'off' notification either. Both directions lost, matching exactly what Joseph reported.

**Fix, 2026-08-12 07:40 MST:** changed the automation's `mode: single` → `mode: restart`. A newer trigger now cancels the in-flight delay and restarts from the latest transition, so rapid back-and-forth collapses into one notification reflecting the final settled state instead of both getting silently lost. Deployed the same way as the prior fixes (YAML syntax-checked, `scp`'d, `docker restart homeassistant`, confirmed `healthy`/no config errors/automation still registered).

**Joseph's third live test, 2026-08-12 ~07:44 MST — off then on, zero notifications again, but a different cause this time.** Checked the automation's Trace view directly (Settings > Automations > Traveling Mode - Unexpected TV Activity > Traces) on Joseph's own request path — "No traces found," meaning the trigger never matched at all, not that it matched and failed downstream. Cross-referenced against `docker logs`: the real state transitions (10:05:58, 10:06:26) landed while HA was still finishing its own startup from the `mode: restart` deploy's container restart — a burst of unrelated SmartThings light entity-setup errors (`KeyError: Capability.SWITCH`, pre-existing issue with some SmartThings lights, unrelated to this card) and recorder DB session-recovery warnings were still running as late as 10:06:27, a full minute-plus after Docker's healthcheck had already reported "healthy." Docker's healthcheck only confirms the web server responds, not that every integration/automation listener has finished wiring up — so the real test landed in a startup race, not a logic bug. No code change needed; confirmed by waiting for HA to be genuinely settled (13+ min uptime, no further restarts) and re-testing.

**Fourth live test, 2026-08-12 ~07:52-07:54 MST — clean pass, both directions.** TV on at 10:21:42 → "on" notification received. TV off at 10:24:17 → "off" notification received. HA fully settled (no restart since 10:05:20), no startup contention this time. This is the first fully clean end-to-end pass since the mode:restart fix.

**Side question from Joseph, same session: should the Samsung TV also be exposed as its own HA device** (rather than relying on the Chromecast as a power-state proxy)? Discussed, not pursued as part of this card — Joseph confirmed the TV is not currently registered in his SmartThings account, so the low-effort "enable exposure on the existing SmartThings integration" path (same pattern as the salt-sensor switches) isn't available; the only route would be HA's separate native Samsung TV integration (added by IP, one-time pairing prompt on the TV). Worth a future card if Joseph wants a more direct/authoritative signal than the Chromecast's Cast-session state, or wants HA to be able to actually turn the TV off itself on an alert rather than just notifying — not needed for this card's own done-when, which the Chromecast proxy now satisfies.

**Fifth live test, 2026-08-12 ~08:31 MST — the quick-toggle case specifically, the one scenario mode:restart hadn't actually been re-verified against yet** (the earlier quick-bounce test that surfaced the mode:single bug predates the restart fix; every test since then was a well-separated on/off). Pulled the exact sub-second timeline from the recorder DB rather than trusting the outcome alone: TV went `playing`→`off` at **10:31:04.852**, starting the 'off' branch's 2-minute countdown, then back to `idle` just **4.68 seconds later** at **10:31:09.531** — under `mode: restart`, this cancelled the in-flight 'off' countdown and started a fresh one for 'on'. State stayed on-ish continuously after that; at the 'on' branch's recheck (~10:33:09) it was still on, so the "on" notification fired — exactly what Joseph received. The original 'off' branch's own recheck (due ~10:33:04) never ran, since it was superseded 4.7s after starting — correctly explains zero 'off' notification. Mechanism confirmed by data, not just a correct-looking result.

**Done when:** a real on/off cycle (both a normal, well-separated toggle and a quick back-and-forth within 2 minutes) produces the correct notification(s) with no drops and no false alerts. **Met** — both the well-separated case (fourth test) and the quick-toggle case (fifth test, above) now confirmed against real sub-second data, on top of the earlier confirmed handling of the connectivity-blip false positive.

**Closed out 2026-08-12 ~08:35 MST on Joseph's go-ahead.** Original root cause was never conclusively found despite exhausting every source checkable (HA automations, JCTsh/ESP32 logs, HA recorder history, Google Home cast activity, TV-native timers, SmartThings routines, other-person access) across the full July 25–Aug 11 trip window — Joseph's call to pivot from root-cause elimination to detection/alerting rather than keep chasing it. The resulting `Traveling Mode - Unexpected TV Activity` automation went through five rounds of real live testing and three real bugs found/fixed along the way (a Cast-integration connectivity-blip false positive, a `mode: single` dropped-notification bug, and one false alarm traced to HA's own startup race rather than the automation) — each one caught by Joseph's own testing, not by config validation alone, consistent with this project's verification standard elsewhere. Reopens under a fresh card if a repeat isn't caught live, or if the TV-as-its-own-device idea (noted above) gets picked up later.

**Related:** CARD-0078 (NetAlertX network-presence entities referenced during investigation), CARD-0098 (Traveling Lights, the toggle this card's alert automation is gated on), `core/homeassistant/automations.yaml`.

### CARD-0149 · [enhancement] [photo-quality-review] Retain historical report.json snapshots for comparison
**Status:** Done

**Raised 2026-08-11**, after Joseph asked about a rescan's completion notification (38,258 duplicate groups) and wanted to know whether that was more than the previous scan turned up -- there was no way to tell, since `scan.js` overwrites `report.json` in place on every run, with no history kept.

**Scope: retention only, not comparison.** Joseph explicitly deferred the diff/comparison half ("38,258 duplicate groups (312 new since last scan)" in the notification) to build later -- this card is just making sure the data exists to compare against, not building the comparison itself.

**Design:** confirmed `groupKey()` (`server.js`) is already stable across rescans -- it's the sorted set of member asset IDs, not scan order or position, so two snapshots really can be diffed meaningfully once this exists. Before `scan.js` overwrites `report.json`, move the existing one into a new `data/report-history/` subdirectory under a filename timestamped from *that report's own* `generatedAt` field (not "now" -- "now" is when it's being retired, not when it was actually generated). `report.json` stays the one filename the app reads; nothing else in `server.js` changes. Deliberately no pruning/retention cap for now -- each snapshot is ~36MB and scans look ad-hoc/infrequent (this was the first rescan since the app's original build), so it would take dozens of scans before size is worth worrying about; simpler to add a cap later if it actually becomes a problem than to guess at a number now.

**Done when:** `scan.js` archives the previous `report.json` into `data/report-history/` (timestamped from its own `generatedAt`) before writing a new one, deployed to the M8, and verified with a real scan run that the history file lands correctly and `report.json`/the app itself are unaffected.

**Built 2026-08-11.** New `archivePreviousReport()` in `scan.js`, called right before the final `fs.writeFile(REPORT_PATH, ...)`: reads the existing `report.json`, pulls its `generatedAt` (falls back to current time if the file's malformed/older-format), then `fs.rename`s it into `data/report-history/report-<timestamp>.json` -- a same-filesystem move, not a copy, so no need to duplicate a ~36MB file on disk just to relocate it.

**Verified in isolation against real Node on the M8, not just "code looks right"** -- `scan.js` itself runs a full ~12.6-minute real scan with no way to unit-test just this one function in place (no `require.main` guard, importing it kicks off the whole scan), so the exact function body was run standalone against synthetic data in a scratch directory: (1) first-ever scan, no existing `report.json` -- no-op, no error; (2) normal case, valid `generatedAt` -- correctly archived as `report-2026-08-10T13-38-44.864Z.json`, original `report.json` confirmed gone; (3) malformed JSON -- falls back to a current-time stamp rather than crashing; (4) repeated archiving -- no filename collisions, all snapshots preserved distinctly. Deployed to `~/photo-quality-review/scan.js` on the M8 (syntax-checked with real `node -c` first), test scratch directory cleaned up afterward.

**Closed out 2026-08-11 on Joseph's go-ahead -- "leave it, it'll run naturally."** Not yet exercised end-to-end against a real scan run (`scan.js` isn't a persistent service, so no restart needed either -- it just takes effect on the next invocation); deliberately not forced today given the isolated test already covers the actual logic faithfully and a full run costs ~12.6 minutes. Real end-to-end proof arrives the next time a rescan runs naturally -- reopens under a fresh card if the history file doesn't land correctly then.

**Related:** CARD-0028 (the review app this extends), CARD-0148 (same component, prior round), `components/photo-quality-review/scan.js`.

### CARD-0148 · [bug] [photo-quality-review] Confirm & Delete and auto-select are both slow -- redundant/blocking work, not real API limits
**Status:** Done

**Raised 2026-08-11**, from Joseph asking how to run two instances of the review app -- the real motivation turned out to be that Confirm & Delete takes a long time, which surfaced a second, related slowness in auto-select once diagnosed. Two independent root causes found by reading the actual code (not guessed):

**1. Confirm & Delete (`server.js` `/api/confirm`).** The per-item delete loop is fully sequential: `await immich.deleteAsset(...)` then `await deletionLog.logDeletion(...)` for every item, one at a time. `deleteAsset` is a fast local call (M8 -> its own Immich container), but `logDeletion` includes `await postToSheet(record)` -- a real internet round-trip to Google Apps Script per item. The code's own comment already establishes this POST is best-effort ("A Sheet POST failure must never block or roll back an already-confirmed Immich delete") and its failure is already caught and just logged -- so awaiting it before moving to the next item buys nothing, it's just blocking for no correctness reason.

**2. Auto-select (`public/review.js` `maybeAutoSelectGroup`).** Once both members' Motion Photo status and album membership are known for a 2-member duplicate group, an unambiguous case gets auto-decided -- fires `/api/decide/duplicate` then `refreshTally()`. On a page with dozens of groups, many can auto-select in a tight burst as badge fetches (already concurrency-capped at 6) resolve. Each auto-select independently triggers a full `refreshTally()` -> `/api/preview` -> `pendingDeletions()`, which re-reads and re-parses `report.json` (whole-library scan data), the entire deletion-log CSV, and `decisions.json` from disk, then loops the whole library -- on *every single call*, no caching. 20-30 auto-selects landing close together means 20-30 redundant full-library reloads, competing with the still-in-flight badge fetches on the same single-threaded Node server.

**Fix, agreed with Joseph:**
1. Stop awaiting the Sheet POST inline in the confirm loop -- fire it, keep the same catch-and-warn behavior via `.catch()` instead of blocking try/catch. Add a small bounded concurrency (e.g. 5) on the main per-item loop as a safety margin, not because Immich itself is currently slow.
2. Debounce `refreshTally()` during auto-select bursts -- coalesce multiple auto-selects landing close together into one refresh shortly after the last one, instead of one full expensive refresh per group.

**Done when:** both fixes are deployed to the M8 and verified against real behavior (a real Confirm & Delete batch and a real page with multiple auto-selectable groups), not just "code looks right" -- matching this project's own verification standard elsewhere. Server-side caching of `pendingDeletions()`'s disk reads is a known further optimization, deliberately out of scope here unless the debounce alone doesn't get far enough.

**Built 2026-08-11:**
1. `deletion-log.js`'s `logDeletion()` no longer awaits `postToSheet()` -- fires it with a `.catch()` instead, same warn-on-failure behavior, off the critical path.
2. `server.js`'s `/api/confirm` delete loop now runs through a small `mapWithConcurrency` (limit 5) instead of a plain sequential `for` loop, mirroring `scan.js`'s own existing helper of the same shape.
3. `review.js`'s `maybeAutoSelectGroup()` now calls a new `scheduleTallyRefreshDebounced()` (200ms) instead of `refreshTally()` directly, so a burst of auto-selects collapses into one tally refresh instead of one per group.

All three syntax-checked with real `node -c` on the M8 (no local Node available on Joseph's machine) before deploying. `server.js`/`deletion-log.js` deployed and require a `sudo systemctl restart photo-quality-review` to take effect (handed to Joseph to run interactively -- this session's tools can't supply a sudo password over SSH); `review.js` is served statically and takes effect on next page load, no restart needed.

**Addendum, same session:** Joseph also asked to add the photo's taken-date to the review page itself (unrelated to the performance fix, folded into this card rather than opened separately since it's the same file/page and came up in the same breath -- flag if you'd rather it be its own card). `fileCreatedAt` was already present on every item (comes straight through from Immich's own asset record via `routes/immich.js`'s `buildPathIndex`) but never rendered anywhere. Added a `formatPhotoDate()` helper (viewer's own local timezone, `toLocaleDateString`) and wired it into both `duplicateMeta()` (duplicate-group thumbnails) and `renderSingle()` (broken/blurry thumbnails). Deployed; no server restart needed (static file).

**Service restarted 2026-08-11, confirmed `active`** -- `server.js`/`deletion-log.js` fixes are now genuinely live, not just deployed to disk.

**Confirm & Delete exercised for real, 2026-08-11 ~13:00 MST -- Joseph ran several real batches from 2017 duplicates, reported "lightning fast."** Speed alone doesn't prove correctness given the concurrency + fire-and-forget changes, so verified each leg independently rather than trusting the feel of it:
- **Immich delete:** queried the Immich API directly for 3 asset IDs from the batch -- all 3 confirmed `isTrashed: true`. The concurrency change didn't cause anything to silently skip.
- **Local CSV log:** `/mnt/photo-library/deletion-log.csv` shows a dense cluster of new rows, all timestamped within the same second matching the batch. `appendLocalCsv()` is still `await`ed per item regardless of concurrency, so this was never actually at risk.
- **Google Sheet log:** the one leg that's fire-and-forget, and the one thing not directly verifiable from the M8 (no read access to the Sheet from here) -- Joseph pasted the Sheet's own last-10-rows, which match the local CSV's entries exactly (same filenames/timestamps/asset IDs/reasons). Confirms the fire-and-forget POST isn't silently dropping rows.
- No `Google Sheet POST failed` warnings anywhere in the service's journal since the restart (this app does no per-request logging otherwise, so silence here specifically means no failures fired, not an absence of monitoring).

Fix 1 (Confirm & Delete) is now fully verified end-to-end against real data, not just "code looks right."

**Closed out 2026-08-11 13:10 MST on Joseph's go-ahead.** Fix 2 (auto-select debounce) and the photo-date addition were deployed and syntax-checked but not separately re-exercised against real behavior the way Fix 1 was -- both are low-risk, small, self-contained changes (a debounce timer and a new display-only field), and Joseph chose to close rather than hold the card open for that. Reopens under a fresh card if either turns out not to behave as expected live.

**Related:** CARD-0028 (the review app this extends), `components/photo-quality-review/server.js`, `components/photo-quality-review/routes/deletion-log.js`, `components/photo-quality-review/public/review.js`.

### CARD-0147 · [idea] [hike-izer] Hike-izer iterative improvement for hike of Aug 10, 2026
**Status:** Done

**Raised 2026-08-10 06:47 MST**, from real observations reviewing today's hike page. Five distinct asks, interviewed same session:

**A. Home link on hike summary pages.** Add a link/button on the hike summary page back to the hikes home/calendar page — currently no way to navigate back without editing the URL by hand.

**B. Map click-to-expand.** Clicking the Route Map opens a full-size modal/lightbox overlay of the same map, for better observability of route/sun-gadget/travel-arrow detail than the normal inline size allows. **Deliberately map-only, no Elevation & Speed chart hover-sync in the expanded view** (Joseph's call, interviewed) — keeping that pairing functional in the modal would either require also carving out modal space for the chart (working against the whole point of maximizing map size) or firing sync events at an on-page chart hidden behind the modal backdrop (invisible, pointless). The modal is a bigger look at the map alone.

**C. "NEW" marker on the Wildlife Heard table.** Mark any species in a hike's own Wildlife Heard table with a small "NEW" badge/pill (Joseph's call on style, interviewed) when that hike is the species' first-ever appearance — i.e. `wildlife_life_list.json`'s `first_heard_file_stem` for that species equals the current hike's own `file_stem`. Distinguishes "first time we've ever heard this bird on any hike" from an established species just showing up again.

**D. Life-list hikes-observed-on count — already exists, no work needed.** Checked directly (Claude, same session) before writing this card rather than assume: `wildlife_life_list.py`'s `update_from_hike()` already maintains a deduplicated `hikes` list per species (idempotent — re-processing the same hike via step 1 then step 2 never double-counts), and `build_wildlife_index.py` already renders `len(e['hikes'])` as a "Hikes" column on `wildlife.html` — distinct from the Wildlife Heard table's own per-hike detection count. This was built as part of CARD-0142 and is already live. Kept as a line item here only for traceability against the original ask, not because anything needs building.

**E. Direction-of-travel arrows (CARD-0085 Part B) — visual rework.** Currently plain rotated markers directly on the route line. Rework to: smaller, lighter stroke weight, and offset to run parallel with the track rather than sitting on top of it (Joseph's call, interviewed: pick a consistent offset rule — e.g. perpendicular to each arrow's own local travel bearing — rather than a single fixed screen-space side; it's fine if the *visual* side varies naturally as the route curves, as long as the rule itself is consistent). This is a refinement of already-built code (`_TRAVEL_ARROW_SVG`/`_select_travel_arrow_points()` in `build_hike_map.py`), not new scope — CARD-0085 itself stays focused on the underlying data pipeline and is unaffected.

**Done when:** all four real work items (A, B, C, E — D needs no action) are built, deployed to the M8, and verified against the live Aug 10, 2026 hike page (or the most recently published hike if that one's already been superseded by further edits).

**Built, deployed, and structurally verified against the real Aug 10, 2026 hike, 2026-08-10 07:20 MST.**
- **A:** `.top-nav` link added to `templating.py`'s hike-summary header (`&larr; All Hikes` &rarr; `index.html`), reusing `build_wildlife_index.py`'s own existing nav-link convention/CSS verbatim rather than inventing a second style.
- **B:** click-to-expand implemented by physically relocating the *same* Leaflet map instance's DOM container into a modal on open (not building/keeping a second map in sync) — a Leaflet control button (`bottomright`, the one open corner: `topright` is the sun gadget, `topleft` is Leaflet's own zoom control) triggers it. `invalidateSize()` + re-`fitBounds()` on both open and close, since Leaflet doesn't notice a container move/resize on its own and the aspect ratio genuinely changes between the small inline card and the large modal.
- **C:** "NEW" badge added to `birdnet_table_rows()`/the Wildlife Heard row markup. **Real bug found and fixed during verification, not just built clean:** the first implementation checked "is this species absent from the life list" -- correct logic for a hike's *first-ever* render (confirmed `render_html()` always runs before `wildlife_life_list.update_from_hike()` in `generation.py`'s own pipeline), but breaks on any *regeneration* of an already-processed hike, which this project does routinely (CARD-0140/144/0085 all did it) -- by the time of a regen, that hike's own species are already merged into the life list, so every one of them would wrongly read "already known" forever after, silently defeating the whole feature on exactly the kind of re-render this session was already doing. Fixed by comparing `first_heard_file_stem` to the current hike's own `file_stem` instead -- a stable, idempotent fact recorded once, correct regardless of how many times a page is rebuilt (matches the card's own original interview wording, which was right the first time).
- **E:** stroke-width 2.5 -> 1.75, icon 18px -> 14px, added `opacity: 0.85`, lighter drop-shadow. Perpendicular offset achieved via CSS transform order alone (`rotate(bearing) translateX(9px)`, translate applied first/innermost) -- no real lat/lon geo-math needed: the pre-rotation local-space translate lands at `bearing+90deg` in real/screen space once the rotation carries it along, i.e. consistently perpendicular to each arrow's own travel direction, "beside the track" rather than on it, varying in absolute compass terms exactly as the route curves (Joseph's own stated tolerance).

**Verification used today's real hike as the test case throughout, not synthetic data** -- confirmed live against `2026-08-10_hike-summary.html` (regenerated in place, reusing the already-fetched photos manifest and re-parsing the still-present staged BirdNET zip, no new Immich/Claude API cost): home link present and `index.html` target reachable (200); modal/expand-control markup present; arrow CSS/offset values present (`stroke-width="1.75"`, `iconSize: [14, 14]`, `translateX(9px)`); and for C specifically, cross-checked the life list directly and found two species genuinely first-heard today (Northern Flicker, Green Frog -- both `first_heard_file_stem: 2026-08-10`, `hikes` count 1) versus 8 others from earlier hikes -- the regenerated page's NEW badge landed on exactly those two rows and no others, a real positive/negative test rather than just confirming the markup exists. **Not yet visually confirmed in a live browser** (the automated browser tool has been unreliable this session, same recurring caveat as CARD-0085's own gadget work) -- worth Joseph's own look, especially for B's modal open/close feel and E's arrow legibility at real map zoom, before calling this fully done.

**Two real bugs found live in B (modal), fixed 2026-08-10 07:29 MST -- exactly the kind of thing structural verification alone can't catch, only an actual look could:**
- **"Map expanded but the route didn't"** -- the modal box grew, but the map/route rendering inside stayed the same size, floating in a bigger empty container. Root cause: `invalidateSize()`/`fitBounds()` were called via a bare `setTimeout(fn, 0)` after the DOM move + CSS display change, which doesn't reliably guarantee the browser has actually finished layout on the just-shown, just-resized container by the time it fires -- Leaflet ended up computing against a stale size. Fixed with a forced synchronous reflow instead (`void container.offsetHeight` immediately after the move/display change, before calling refit()) -- reading a layout property forces the browser to compute layout right then, a standard, reliable technique for this exact class of bug, rather than hoping a timeout won this race.
- **Blank gap in the bottom half of the modal.** Root cause: the map's fill-height chain (`.map-modal` -> `.map-modal-container` -> the moved `.hike-map` div) relied on `height: 100%` cascading through several nested divs -- fragile and, in this case, wrong somewhere in the chain (not fully traced by inspection alone). Replaced with an explicit flexbox fill pattern (`.map-modal` as `display:flex; flex-direction:column`, `.map-modal-container` as `flex: 1 1 auto; min-height: 0`) -- a more robust, harder-to-get-wrong way to say "fill the remaining space," and sidesteps percentage-height resolution edge cases entirely. `min-height: 0` matters here specifically: flex items default to `min-height: auto`, which can stop a child with its own internal sizing (a Leaflet map) from actually shrinking/growing to fill a flex parent -- a well-known flexbox gotcha.

Both fixes deployed and re-verified against a fresh regeneration of the same real `2026-08-10_hike-summary.html` -- forced-reflow JS and flex CSS both confirmed present in the output, NEW badge still correctly on exactly the same 2 rows (no regression from the unrelated CSS/JS changes). **Still not visually confirmed in a live browser** -- these fixes are reasoned from the actual bug mechanics (confirmed real Leaflet/flexbox gotchas, not guesses) and structurally verified, but the modal's actual on-screen feel remains unconfirmed until Joseph looks again.

**First real screenshot, 2026-08-10 07:37 MST -- confirms both modal fixes above actually worked** (route shows real street-level zoom, no floating-in-empty-space; modal box ends right after the Leaflet attribution line, no visible blank gap). Surfaced a separate, genuine issue instead: event markers (photo/observation/bird-sighting circles, `.map-marker-icon`) read too large and overlap heavily where several land close together on the route -- a real cluster of bird-sighting markers in the screenshot was nearly indistinguishable as separate items. Also asked whether the white circle background is even needed. **Recommendation given and applied:** keep the circle -- these are thin stroke-only line-art icons (not filled shapes) against Thunderforest's visually busy "Outdoors" basemap (roads, labels, green space), and dropping the background would likely cost real legibility -- but shrink everything. `.map-marker-icon` 26px -> 20px (inner SVG 16px -> 12px, border 1.5px -> 1.25px, lighter shadow), matching `L.divIcon`'s own `iconSize`/`iconAnchor` in `build_hike_map.py`. Deployed, regenerated against the same real hike page, confirmed present in the output. Not yet re-screenshotted to confirm the overlap actually reads better at the new size.

**Correction to the "no visible blank gap" claim above, 2026-08-10 07:50 MST -- it was wrong.** Joseph's own laptop screenshot tool couldn't capture the modal overlay at all (only whatever was behind it), so he took a real phone photo of the screen instead -- which showed a real, still-present blank gap below the map, plus asked to "expand the map vertically so it fills the modal box." **Real root cause, found by actually reading the competing selectors rather than re-guessing:** the modal markup is nested *inside* the Route Map's own `<section>` (inside `.hike-visuals`, since `build_map_html()`'s return value -- inline card + modal together -- gets embedded as one block by `render_html()`) -- so even after JS relocates the map div into the modal, it's still a genuine DOM descendant of `.hike-visuals`, `position: fixed` notwithstanding (CSS descendant selectors match by DOM ancestry, not visual/layout escape -- the exact wrong assumption the earlier "no gap" claim rested on). `.hike-visuals .hike-map`'s own responsive rule (`height: 15rem`, active at typical laptop widths) has the *same* specificity as the plain 2-class selector this fix used, and is declared later in the stylesheet -- so it silently won the tie and clamped the map to 15rem regardless of the modal's real size. Fixed with a 3-class selector (`.map-modal-backdrop .map-modal-container .hike-map`), unambiguously higher specificity, so it wins regardless of source order or which other responsive rule also matches.

**Marker overlap -- both raised options from the interview applied together (Joseph: "1 and 2"), not either/or:**
1. Shrunk further, 20px -> 16px (inner SVG 12px -> 9px, border 1.25px -> 1px) -- same size/CSS-only direction as the first pass.
2. **New: server-side marker declutter/spread** (`templating._declutter_markers()`), since the remaining overlap wasn't purely a size problem -- several BirdNET detections logged seconds apart while stationary land at genuinely near-identical GPS positions, which no amount of shrinking alone would separate. Groups markers within `MARKER_CLUSTER_RADIUS_M` (8m real-world distance, haversine -- not a fixed lat/lon epsilon, which would be wrong at different latitudes since longitude degrees shrink toward the poles) and spreads each group evenly around its own centroid at `MARKER_SPREAD_RADIUS_M` (5m) -- a simple deterministic version of the "spiderfy" technique clustering map libraries use, computed in plain Python (no new JS dependency) since every marker's real lat/lon is already known server-side at render time.

All three fixes deployed and verified against a fresh regeneration of the same real `2026-08-10_hike-summary.html`: the higher-specificity selector, 16px markers, and `iconSize:[16,16]` all confirmed present in the output; NEW-species badge still correct (no regression). Decluttering verified by computing real pairwise distances between every marker in the final output -- no two markers closer than ~8.5m (the cluster threshold), and the three pairs that WERE clustered now sit ~10-12m apart, matching almost exactly what two points spread to opposite sides of a 5m-radius circle around a shared centroid should produce -- a real, checked result, not just "the code looks right."

**Real photo of the declutter fix showed the overlap barely changed, 2026-08-10 08:05 MST -- root cause was the metric itself, not the math.** The 8m/5m real-world-meter thresholds were correctly computed (per the pairwise-distance check above), but real-world meters translate to a *fixed* pixel distance only at a given zoom level -- the same 10-12m separation that looked adequate at the modal's high zoom was still visually cramped at the inline card's lower zoom, since more real-world meters fit into the same pixel space when zoomed out. A server-side, meters-based fix can't be zoom-aware, because rendering happens once at generation time while zoom happens live in the browser. Joseph proposed the fix directly: move markers off the route into open map space with a leader line back to the real point on hover, which decouples "far enough apart to read" from "real-world distance" entirely -- offered a choice between that and a separate strip/legend layout; Joseph picked "callout pins in open map space."

**Rebuilt as a client-side pixel-space callout system, 2026-08-10 08:15 MST -- `_declutter_markers()`/`_haversine_m()` removed from `templating.py` entirely, replaced by `layoutEventMarkers()` in `build_hike_map.py`.** Runs in the browser on `zoomend`/`moveend`, so it's correct at every zoom level, not just the one assumed at render time:
- Each marker's real lat/lon is projected to its current on-screen pixel position (`latLngToContainerPoint`), then offset perpendicular to the route's local bearing at that point (found by nearest-track-point lookup, same perpendicular-via-CSS-transform trick as the E travel arrows) by `MARKER_OFFSET_PX` (22px).
- If that offset position is still within `MARKER_CLUSTER_PX` (18px) of an already-placed marker, it steps further out along the same perpendicular direction in `MARKER_SPREAD_STEP_PX` (16px) increments (up to 8 tries) until clear -- markers can now spread as far as needed since they're no longer constrained to sit near the real point.
- On hover, a dashed leader line (`marker-leader-line`) and a small highlight dot (`marker-real-point`) are drawn from the callout back to the actual real-world point on the track, added/removed live so they don't clutter the resting state.
- Also addressed in the same round: continuous zoom (`zoomSnap: 0.25` on the Leaflet map init, replacing the default fixed integer zoom levels), per Joseph's question "how about a zoom that is continuous rather than a set zoom value?"; and the sun-direction gadget circle restyled smaller and to actually resemble a sun (2.75rem -> 2.1rem, `var(--surface)` flat fill -> gold `radial-gradient` with amber border/glow, arrow color `var(--accent)` -> dark amber `#6b4416`) per the mid-round ask "make the circle smaller and resemble a sun."

Verified structurally against a fresh regeneration of the same real `2026-08-10_hike-summary.html`: `zoomSnap: 0.25` present; `layoutEventMarkers`/`MARKER_OFFSET_PX`/`marker-leader-line` all present (9 occurrences); restyled `.sun-gadget` CSS (radial-gradient, amber border) present; NEW-species badge still correct on exactly the same 2 rows (no regression); page live, HTTP 200. Also confirmed via a standalone script generating real HTML with test chart/marker data and checking brace/paren/bracket balance in the embedded `<script>` block (all balanced) plus manually reading the generated `layoutEventMarkers()` output for logic correctness, since `py_compile` alone only validates the Python side, not the JS it's templating. **Not yet visually confirmed in a live browser or real photo** -- same recurring caveat as the rest of this card's work; worth Joseph's own look at the callout/leader-line behavior, continuous zoom feel, and sun gadget appearance before this round is called done.

**Two more real issues found on Joseph's own look, fixed 2026-08-10 08:30 MST:**
- **Leader line obscured by its own tooltip.** The callout hover tooltip was fixed at `direction: "top"`, but the leader line runs from the callout back toward the real point in whatever direction that happens to be -- "almost all cases" it ran roughly upward too, so the tooltip box sat right on top of it. Fixed by pointing the tooltip along the *same* `dir` vector already used to place the callout (away from the route) instead of a fixed side -- since the leader line runs the opposite way (`-dir`, back toward the real point), the tooltip is now guaranteed clear of its own line regardless of which way the route curves at that spot, the same "derive it from the actual geometry, not a fixed assumption" fix pattern as several of this card's earlier rounds.
- **Sun gadget restyled to actually look like a sun**, per a reference image Joseph linked (a classic icon: gold circle plus radiating pointed rays), not just a plain warm circle. Added an 8-point sunburst (`.sun-gadget-rays`, orange `#f7941e`, points computed offline into a static polygon, not live math) behind the existing gold circle. The layering is done via DOM sibling order, not z-index -- a child element's own background can never paint behind its parent's, so the circle moved from being `.sun-gadget`'s own background to a second sibling (`.sun-gadget-core`) painted after the rays, letting the spike tips show past its edge while the opaque circle covers the rest of the burst.

Both deployed and verified against a fresh regeneration: `tooltipDir` logic and the new `.sun-gadget-rays`/`.sun-gadget-core` structure both confirmed present; NEW-species badge still correct (no regression); page live, HTTP 200.

**A third real issue, fixed 2026-08-10 08:45 MST -- travel arrows weren't reliably parallel to the drawn track.** The arrow's rotation had been using each point's stored `travel_bearing_deg` (a real GPS-reported heading) ever since CARD-0085 built it. Joseph's explicit requirement -- "should always be parallel to the track, but not touch the track" -- is a promise about what's *drawn* on the map, and a device's instantaneous heading can genuinely diverge from the straight segment between two plotted points (sensor noise, a brief pause, a tight switchback between samples), which no amount of styling would fix since the two numbers just don't always agree. Fixed by deriving the rotation from the route polyline's own local on-screen tangent instead (`tangentAngleDeg()` in `build_hike_map.py`, reading straight from `points[]` via `latLngToContainerPoint` -- the exact same data and technique the event-marker callouts already use for their own perpendicular offset), so the arrow is now geometrically guaranteed to match what's actually drawn, not just usually close to it. `travel_bearing_deg` is kept only as the *gate* for which points get an arrow at all (real GPS-reported heading confirms it's a genuine waypoint) -- its numeric value is no longer used for the angle. The existing perpendicular-offset mechanism (CSS `rotate() translateX(9px)`, unchanged) still keeps the arrow off the line itself, and is now built on the same guaranteed-accurate angle.

Deployed and verified against a fresh regeneration: `tangentAngleDeg` defined and wired to each arrow (`tangentAngleDeg(t.idx)`), the old `t.bearing`-based rotation fully removed from the output, NEW-species badge still correct, page live, HTTP 200.

**Closed out 2026-08-10 08:50 MST on Joseph's go-ahead.** Every item this card tracked (A/B/C/E, D needing no action) has been built, deployed, and iterated against real feedback from today's actual hike across several rounds -- including catching and fixing real bugs (modal route-scaling, modal blank gap, marker overlap being fundamentally zoom-dependent, tooltip-over-leader-line, and this final travel-arrow angle-source bug) that structural verification alone couldn't have surfaced, only Joseph's own screenshots/photos and explicit requirements could. The last two rounds (leader-line/sun gadget, travel-arrow angle) are structurally verified the same way as everything else on this card but not yet re-confirmed in a fresh screenshot -- if either doesn't look right live, it reopens under a fresh card rather than this one, per the project's own convention for closed work.

**Reopened 2026-08-10 09:05 MST -- F. Sortable Wildlife Life List columns.** Joseph's call to fold this into this card rather than open a new one, since it's a small, self-contained addition to the same page family (`wildlife.html`, `build_wildlife_index.py`) this card's other items already touched. Click any of the four column headers (Common Name, Scientific Name, First Heard, Hikes) to sort ascending/descending, with a small triangle indicator on the active column. `build_wildlife_index.py` was explicitly "deliberately zero-JS" per its own docstring (modeled on `build_calendar_index.py`) -- this is a scoped, documented exception, since sorting genuinely needs client-side interactivity that no static render can provide. Implementation: each `<td>` carries a `data-sort-value` (lowercased for the two text columns, the raw `YYYY-MM-DD` `first_heard_file_stem` for First Heard -- already lexicographically sortable as-is, no separate date parsing needed -- and the raw integer for Hikes), each `<th>` carries a `data-sort-type` ("text" or "number") so the click handler knows whether to `localeCompare` or subtract; clicking the same column again reverses direction. Deployed and verified against a fresh regeneration of the real `wildlife.html` (33 species): 132 `data-sort-value` attributes present (33 x 4 columns), all 4 `data-sort-type` headers present, script brace-balanced, page live (HTTP 200). **Not yet visually confirmed** -- worth Joseph's own click-through before this closes again.

**G. Same two features on the per-hike Wildlife Heard (BirdNET) table, plus a Life List link in its header** -- Joseph's follow-up while F was landing. Brings the per-hike table in line with the cross-hike life-list page:
- Wikipedia link on each species' common name, reusing `build_wildlife_index.wikipedia_url()` directly (renamed from `_wikipedia_url` -- no longer private, now a cross-module helper) rather than a second copy of the same URL-building one-liner. `templating.py` already imports `build_hike_map`/`build_hike_chart` as sibling modules the same way (documented in its own header comment), so importing `build_wildlife_index` too follows an established pattern, not a new one.
- Click-to-sort on all four columns (Species, Count, Confidence, Time), scoped specifically to `#birdnet-table` -- the hiking-observations table elsewhere on the same page also uses the shared `.obs-table` class but isn't sortable, so both the CSS (cursor/hover/arrow) and the sort script target the id, not the class, to avoid implying every table on the page is clickable. Confidence sorts on the raw percent int (`confidence_pct`), not the displayed `"83%"` string; Time sorts on the raw ISO timestamp (`time_iso`), not the displayed `"8:01 AM"` string, since AM/PM text doesn't order correctly around noon. Unlike F's script, this one starts with no column marked active (`state.col = -1`) rather than assuming column 0 -- the table's real initial order is birdnet.py's own first-detection-time grouping, not a simple single-column sort, so claiming Species was "already sorted ascending" would have been a false default.
- `<h2>Wildlife Heard (BirdNET)</h2>` wrapped in a new `.section-header` flex row with a "Life List &rarr;" link to `wildlife.html` -- a lighter-weight inline pill than the page-top `.top-nav` link (A), sized to sit next to a section heading rather than at the very top of the page.

Deployed and verified against a fresh regeneration of the real `2026-08-10_hike-summary.html`: `#birdnet-table` present once, 40 `data-sort-value` attributes (10 species x 4 columns), 10 Wikipedia links, the Life List header link present, NEW-species badge still correct (no regression from the unrelated table changes), all 3 embedded `<script>` blocks on the page brace-balanced, page live (HTTP 200) -- and `wildlife.html` re-verified in the same pass to confirm the `wikipedia_url` rename didn't break its own existing link (33 links, 132 sort attributes, still live). **Not yet visually confirmed** -- same caveat as F.

**Two real bugs found on Joseph's first actual look (screenshot), fixed 2026-08-10 09:20 MST:**
- **Garbled characters in the sort-arrow column headers** (`SCIENTIFIC NAME□9□B2` in the screenshot). Root cause: the sort-arrow CSS (`content: "\2009\25B2"`, meant as CSS's own hex-escape syntax for a thin space + a "▲" triangle) was written inside a *plain* Python string, not a raw one -- Python parsed `\2009`/`\25B2` as its own octal escapes (`\200` = byte 0x80, `\25` = byte 0x15) before the CSS ever reached the browser, silently corrupting the value into control bytes plus leftover literal digits. This existed in both `build_wildlife_index.py` (life list) and `templating.py` (per-hike table) -- same bug, same copy-paste origin, Joseph flagged the hike page had it too before a second screenshot was even needed. Fixed by writing the actual Unicode characters directly in the Python source (thin space + ▲/▼) instead of a CSS-escape string, sidestepping the Python-vs-CSS escape-syntax collision entirely rather than just correcting the escape (e.g. to `\\2009\\25B2`) and leaving the same trap for the next person who copies this pattern.
- **Wildlife names didn't read as links.** `wildlife.html`'s own `td a { color: var(--accent); text-decoration: none; }` styled every link in its table (including the newly-added Wikipedia links) as green, no underline -- a deliberate original design choice for that page, but one Joseph now wants reverted to look like an actual hyperlink, "the standard blue underline... like on the hike page." The hike page has no such override for its plain `<a>` tags (confirmed -- no general `a`/`td a` rule in `templating.py`'s stylesheet), so its own Wikipedia links already render in the browser's default blue/underline; removed the override in `build_wildlife_index.py` entirely so `wildlife.html` now matches that same unstyled-default look rather than adding a second, separate blue/underline rule to maintain.

Both fixes deployed and verified against a fresh regeneration of both real pages: no corrupted 0x80/0x15 bytes in either page's output, `td a` color override confirmed removed from `wildlife.html`, both pages live (HTTP 200).

**Closed out again 2026-08-10 09:25 MST -- Joseph confirmed live.** Every item across the full card (A/B/C/E/F/G, D needing no action) is built, deployed, and now visually confirmed working, including this round's two real bugs (garbled sort-arrow header text, wildlife-name link styling) that only Joseph's own screenshot caught.

**Related:** CARD-0082 (Route Map this extends), CARD-0110 (Elevation & Speed chart, the hover-sync pairing B deliberately excludes from the expanded view), CARD-0085 (travel-direction arrows E reworks the visuals of, not the underlying data), CARD-0142/CARD-0143 (wildlife life list D found already-built), `components/hike-izer/build_hike_map.py`, `components/hike-izer/build_wildlife_index.py`, `components/hike-izer-orchestrator/wildlife_life_list.py`, `components/hike-izer-orchestrator/templating.py`.
